Well... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I used to always do things after my shifts to decompress. I'd paint, or go to a yoga class, or sometimes even salsa dancing. It was just a good way to unwind, you know? 

But lately... I don't know, I just haven't really been doing any of that. I usually just go straight home and sit on the couch. I even find myself ignoring texts from friends because... well, it just doesn't feel worth the effort right now. 

But I'm sure that's just because I'm tired from work and, you know, the pregnancy, right? It's normal to want to just rest more? I hope I don't sound lazy or anything. I just haven't had the same energy for my hobbies lately.

Oh, um... well, I guess I'm still trying to find things? I know I mentioned before that I used to paint or do yoga or salsa dancing after my shifts to decompress... and I really do want to get back to those.

I know I probably sound like a broken record, repeating myself like this. I'm sorry if I'm not giving you a better answer. I really am trying to be more active, I just... I haven't found anything that feels enjoyable lately. Is that okay? I mean, is it normal to just need a break from everything for a little while?

Oh, um... well... I would never, ever actually do anything to hurt myself or the baby. I promise you that. I don't have any sort of plan or anything like that. I know how terrible that would be.

I feel so guilty even admitting that out loud, especially being pregnant. It makes me sound like such a horrible person, doesn't it? I swear I'm not going to do anything, it's just... those thoughts kind of scared me. I hope you don't think less of me for saying that... is it okay that I shared that with you?

Oh, um... well, I haven't stepped on a scale recently, mostly because I'm a little scared to look. I know I'm supposed to be gaining weight for the baby, and I really, really don't want to do anything wrong. But... I guess I might have lost a little bit?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well... I really, really don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm a danger to the baby. I promise I would never, ever do anything to actually hurt myself. I know how important it is to keep the baby safe, and I'm trying so hard to be a good mom already. 

But... I guess... well, this is actually why I said something at my last prenatal appointment. It kind of scared me, honestly. I've just been having these thoughts lately... not about hurting myself! Just... sometimes I catch myself wishing I just wouldn't wake up. Like it would just be easier if I could sleep and not have to face everything. 

I feel so terrible even admitting that. It sounds so selfish, doesn't it? With a baby on the way and a husband who loves me... I should be so happy. I swear I don't have any kind of plan or anything like that. It's just a passing thought sometimes when things feel really heavy. I hope you don't think I'm an awful person for having thoughts like that... is it somewhat normal for the hormones to make you think weird things sometimes?

I just wanted to make sure I was doing the right thing by coming in.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling this heavy, sad pressure?\n\nPatient: Um, it's been about... three weeks? I really hope I'm not making a big deal out of nothing. But it started shortly after I... well... after I found out I was pregnant. \n\nWhich, I mean, is a good thing! I should be absolutely thrilled, right? I feel so guilty even complaining about feeling sad right now, because I know I should just be grateful. But yeah, it's been about three weeks since I found out. Is... is it okay that I'm bringing this up? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing, you know, for the baby and everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I used to always do things after my shifts to decompress. I'd paint, or go to a yoga class, or sometimes even salsa dancing. It was just a good way to unwind, you know? \n\nBut lately... I don't know, I just haven't really been doing any of that. I usually just go straight home and sit on the couch. I even find myself ignoring texts from friends because... well, it just doesn't feel worth the effort right now. \n\nBut I'm sure that's just because I'm tired from work and, you know, the pregnancy, right? It's normal to want to just rest more? I hope I don't sound lazy or anything. I just haven't had the same energy for my hobbies lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the activities you do manage to participate in, like painting or yoga?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm sorry, I probably didn't explain myself very well just a second ago! That's my fault. I actually... I haven't really been managing to do them at all. \n\nLike I was saying, instead of doing the painting or yoga after my shifts, I really do just go straight home and sit on the couch. Even when I think about trying to do something fun, it just... it doesn't feel worth the effort. That's why I've been ignoring those texts from my friends. On the rare chance I do try to pick up a paintbrush, it just feels like a chore instead of being fun, so I just put it away. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound awful. I know I should be keeping up with my friends and staying active... is it okay that I'm just taking a break from it all right now? I promise I'm going to try to get back into a better routine soon.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling guilty about feeling sad after finding out you're pregnant, can you tell me more about that guilt?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's just... I know this baby is a blessing. My husband and I have been wanting this, and... and I should be glowing, right? Everyone says you're supposed to be so happy when you're pregnant. \n\nBut instead, I just feel this heavy cloud over me, and... and then I feel terrible for feeling the cloud. Because what kind of mother feels miserable when she finally gets what she wanted? I just... I worry I'm already failing at this, you know? Like I'm being selfish or ungrateful. \n\nI know I shouldn't think like that. I really am trying to be positive, I promise. It's just hard sometimes. Do... do other women ever feel like this? I just hope I'm not the only one who's ever reacted this way.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess I'm still trying to find things? I know I mentioned before that I used to paint or do yoga or salsa dancing after my shifts to decompress... and I really do want to get back to those. \n\nBut right now, honestly... I just go home and sit on the couch. I still haven't been answering texts from my friends, either. It's just... I try to think about doing something I used to like, and it just doesn't feel worth the effort right now. \n\nI know I probably sound like a broken record, repeating myself like this. I'm sorry if I'm not giving you a better answer. I really am trying to be more active, I just... I haven't found anything that feels enjoyable lately. Is that okay? I mean, is it normal to just need a break from everything for a little while?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you joy or pleasure at all right now?\n\nPatient: Oh... I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm failing this question since I know we just touched on this. I really wish I could give you a better answer, I really do. I know I should be doing things that make me happy, especially right now. \n\nBut like I mentioned, even though I used to love going to salsa dancing, or doing yoga, or painting after my nursing shifts to decompress... right now, there's just nothing that brings me that spark. I just go straight home and sit on the couch. I'm still ignoring all those texts from my friends because... well, none of it feels worth the effort, no matter how much I try to force it. \n\nI promise I'm not trying to be difficult, and I really am trying to snap out of it. Is it okay that I don't have anything bringing me joy at the moment? I hope you're not frustrated with me for just giving you the exact same answer...\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things, like eating a favorite food or taking a warm bath, that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I've tried a few little things like that? I know taking a warm bath is supposed to be really relaxing, especially when you're pregnant, and I did try that the other night. \n\nBut honestly... it just felt like sitting in hot water. I didn't get that relaxed, happy feeling I used to get. And food... I've been trying to eat healthy for the baby, but even if my husband brings home my favorite takeout, it just tastes like... nothing special. \n\nI feel so bad saying this, because I know those little things are supposed to help, and I really am trying to appreciate them. I'm sorry I'm being so negative. I hope it's alright that I'm just not finding much pleasure in anything right now... I'm trying my best, I promise. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I would never, ever actually do anything to hurt myself or the baby. I promise you that. I don't have any sort of plan or anything like that. I know how terrible that would be. \n\nBut... I guess... well, part of the reason I actually said something at my prenatal appointment was because... I've just been having these thoughts lately. Just... sort of wishing I wouldn't wake up in the morning. Like it would just be easier to sleep and not have to face everything. \n\nI feel so guilty even admitting that out loud, especially being pregnant. It makes me sound like such a horrible person, doesn't it? I swear I'm not going to do anything, it's just... those thoughts kind of scared me. I hope you don't think less of me for saying that... is it okay that I shared that with you?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I haven't stepped on a scale recently, mostly because I'm a little scared to look. I know I'm supposed to be gaining weight for the baby, and I really, really don't want to do anything wrong. But... I guess I might have lost a little bit? \n\nI know I should be eating regularly, especially right now, but... most days food just sounds completely unappealing. I'll be at work, and I'll realize halfway through a clinic shift that all I've had all day is just... some crackers or a coffee. \n\nI feel so guilty saying that out loud, especially as a nurse. I know better. I promise I'm going to try harder to force myself to eat, I really am. I hope you're not too disappointed in me... do you think I'm doing a terrible job with this? I really am trying to be good about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... actually, no, not really. I mean, I know working twelve-hour shifts at the clinic and being pregnant is supposed to make you completely exhausted, and I really don't want to sound like I'm bragging or anything! But... I haven't really noticed feeling tired most of the time. \n\nI try really hard to keep my energy up, especially for my patients, and I want to be present when I get home, too. I guess I've just been pushing through? I hope that's okay... I know I probably should be resting more for the baby, and I promise I'll try to sit down more if I need to. But physically, my energy levels have actually been mostly okay. \n\nI hope I'm giving you the right answers... is it weird that I'm not super tired right now?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I hope this doesn't make me sound like a bad nurse, because I really do try to stay on top of everything at the clinic. I promise I'm still getting all my work done. But... I guess I have noticed that I'm moving a little slower lately? \n\nIt's just... everything feels kind of heavy, almost like I'm moving through syrup or something. Even really simple things, like just gathering wound care supplies for a patient or finishing up my charting at the end of the shift... it just seems to take me noticeably longer than it usually would. \n\nI feel awful admitting that, because I know my coworkers rely on me and I don't want to be a burden to anyone. I swear I'm trying my hardest to keep my normal pace. Do you think that's just because of the pregnancy, or... is it really bad that I'm slowing down like that? I'm trying to do better, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I mean... I wouldn't say I feel like a *bad* person. I really try to be a good person, especially to my patients and my husband. I promise I'm always trying to do the right thing. \n\nBut... I guess sometimes I do feel a little... I don't know, maybe like I'm not doing enough? Like with the baby coming, and me not eating right, and slowing down at work... it just makes me feel like I'm failing at everything. Like I'm just not cut out for this, even though I wanted it so much. \n\nI know I shouldn't think like that, and I feel so guilty for even saying it out loud. I know I have so much to be thankful for. I'm sorry, I don't want to sound ungrateful or dramatic. I'm really trying to stay positive, I promise. Do you think it's normal to feel a little inadequate sometimes, especially with the pregnancy? I hope I'm not sounding too awful...\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death frequently?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I really, really don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm a danger to the baby. I promise I would never, ever do anything to actually hurt myself. Like it would just be easier if I could sleep and not have to face everything. \n\nI feel so terrible even admitting that. It sounds so selfish, doesn't it? With a baby on the way and a husband who loves me... I should be so happy. I swear I don't have any kind of plan or anything like that. It's just a passing thought sometimes when things feel really heavy. I hope you don't think I'm an awful person for having thoughts like that... is it somewhat normal for the hormones to make you think weird things sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you been thinking about death often?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or... did I misunderstand the question before? I'm so sorry if I'm not making sense or if I'm repeating myself. I really don't want to waste your time. \n\nLike I was just saying... I promise I don't want to die or anything like that. I really don't. It's just those fleeting thoughts of... maybe it would be easier to just not wake up. But that's it, I swear. I hope I'm answering your questions right... I'm really trying to be helpful. I even find myself ignoring texts from friends because... well, it just doesn't feel worth the effort right now. \n\nBut I'm sure that's just because I'm tired from work and, you know, the pregnancy, right? It's normal to want to just rest more? I hope I don't sound lazy or anything. I just haven't had the same energy for my hobbies lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the activities you do manage to participate in, like painting or yoga?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm sorry, I probably didn't explain myself very well just a second ago! That's my fault. I actually... I haven't really been managing to do them at all. \n\nLike I was saying, instead of doing the painting or yoga after my shifts, I really do just go straight home and sit on the couch. Even when I think about trying to do something fun, it just... it doesn't feel worth the effort. That's why I've been ignoring those texts from my friends. On the rare chance I do try to pick up a paintbrush, it just feels like a chore instead of being fun, so I just put it away. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound awful. I know I should be keeping up with my friends and staying active... is it okay that I'm just taking a break from it all right now? I promise I'm going to try to get back into a better routine soon.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling guilty about feeling sad after finding out you're pregnant, can you tell me more about that guilt?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's just... I know this baby is a blessing. My husband and I have been wanting this, and... and I should be glowing, right? Everyone says you're supposed to be so happy when you're pregnant. \n\nBut instead, I just feel this heavy cloud over me, and... and then I feel terrible for feeling the cloud. Because what kind of mother feels miserable when she finally gets what she wanted? I just... I worry I'm already failing at this, you know? Like I'm being selfish or ungrateful. \n\nI know I shouldn't think like that. I really am trying to be positive, I promise. It's just hard sometimes. Do... do other women ever feel like this? I just hope I'm not the only one who's ever reacted this way.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess I'm still trying to find things? I know I mentioned before that I used to paint or do yoga or salsa dancing after my shifts to decompress... and I really do want to get back to those. \n\nBut right now, honestly... I just go home and sit on the couch. I still haven't been answering texts from my friends, either. It's just... I try to think about doing something I used to like, and it just doesn't feel worth the effort right now. \n\nI know I probably sound like a broken record, repeating myself like this. I'm sorry if I'm not giving you a better answer. I really am trying to be more active, I just... I haven't found anything that feels enjoyable lately. Is that okay? I mean, is it normal to just need a break from everything for a little while?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you joy or pleasure at all right now?\n\nPatient: Oh... I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm failing this question since I know we just touched on this. I really wish I could give you a better answer, I really do. I know I should be doing things that make me happy, especially right now. \n\nBut like I mentioned, even though I used to love going to salsa dancing, or doing yoga, or painting after my nursing shifts to decompress... right now, there's just nothing that brings me that spark. I just go straight home and sit on the couch. I'm still ignoring all those texts from my friends because... well, none of it feels worth the effort, no matter how much I try to force it. \n\nI promise I'm not trying to be difficult, and I really am trying to snap out of it. Is it okay that I don't have anything bringing me joy at the moment? I hope you're not frustrated with me for just giving you the exact same answer...\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things, like eating a favorite food or taking a warm bath, that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I've tried a few little things like that? I know taking a warm bath is supposed to be really relaxing, especially when you're pregnant, and I did try that the other night. \n\nBut honestly... it just felt like sitting in hot water. I didn't get that relaxed, happy feeling I used to get. And food... I've been trying to eat healthy for the baby, but even if my husband brings home my favorite takeout, it just tastes like... nothing special. \n\nI feel so bad saying this, because I know those little things are supposed to help, and I really am trying to appreciate them. I'm sorry I'm being so negative. I hope it's alright that I'm just not finding much pleasure in anything right now... I'm trying my best, I promise. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I would never, ever actually do anything to hurt myself or the baby. I promise you that. I don't have any sort of plan or anything like that. I know how terrible that would be. \n\nBut... I guess... well, part of the reason I actually said something at my prenatal appointment was because... I've just been having these thoughts lately. Just... sort of wishing I wouldn't wake up in the morning. Like it would just be easier to sleep and not have to face everything. \n\nI feel so guilty even admitting that out loud, especially being pregnant. It makes me sound like such a horrible person, doesn't it? I swear I'm not going to do anything, it's just... those thoughts kind of scared me. I hope you don't think less of me for saying that... is it okay that I shared that with you?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I haven't stepped on a scale recently, mostly because I'm a little scared to look. I know I'm supposed to be gaining weight for the baby, and I really, really don't want to do anything wrong. But... I guess I might have lost a little bit? \n\nI know I should be eating regularly, especially right now, but... most days food just sounds completely unappealing. I'll be at work, and I'll realize halfway through a clinic shift that all I've had all day is just... some crackers or a coffee. \n\nI feel so guilty saying that out loud, especially as a nurse. I really am trying to be good about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... actually, no, not really. I'm trying to do better, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I mean... I wouldn't say I feel like a *bad* person. I don't know, maybe like I'm not doing enough? Like with the baby coming, and me not eating right, and slowing down at work... it just makes me feel like I'm failing at everything. Like I'm just not cut out for this, even though I wanted it so much. \n\nI know I shouldn't think like that, and I feel so guilty for even saying it out loud. Oh, um... well... I hope this doesn't make me sound incompetent, because I really do take my job seriously. I know how important it is to be sharp when you're taking care of people. But... I guess lately, things have been a little harder to focus on.

Oh, um... distress? Well... I mean, I'm still making it to work every day. I really am. I know how short-staffed we are, and I'd never want to leave my team hanging. I'm trying so hard to keep everything together.
